Feynman's Non-Relativistic Path Integral Development
This title was summarized by AI from the post below.
View profile for Swapnonil Banerjee, PhD
Swapnonil Banerjee, PhD
5mo  Edited

Feynman’s path integral is used so extensively in relativistic quantum field theory that it’s easy to forget it was originally developed in a non-relativistic setting. In fact, Feynman's thesis (link below), which introduced the path integral technique, was entirely about non-relativistic quantum mechanics, much like the Schrodinger equation. Quoting from his thesis (p. 6): "All of the analysis will apply to non-relativistic systems. The generalization to the relativistic case is not at present known." Feynman developed the non-relativistic path integral starting from the classical action, which yields Newton’s second law through variational calculus. Only later did he extend the Path Integral formalism to the relativistic case.
#physics #quantum #path #integral #relativity #classical
https://lnkd.in/gBFPm3M4


files.untiredwithloving.org

Swapnonil thanks for posting Feynman's thesis.  I have never taken the time to track down and review his thesis but upon quickly reviewing it in your post, here are my brief thoughts.  I find it to exhibit his signature intuitive, conversational style which focuses on physical insight rather than dense mathematical formalism and, in this regard, its style reminds me of his Feynman Lectures.  IMO, his thesis is very "readable" by anyone with a solid graduate education in physics.   I will be adding this to my summer reading list.

Nicolae Sfetcu
Member of CRISFT (Academia Romana), Owner of MultiMedia Publishing

5mo

String theory, a framework that aims to reconcile general relativity and quantum mechanics, holds a unique position in the field of quantum gravity. In quantum field theory, the main obstacle is the occurrence of the untreatable infinities in the interactions of the particles due to the possibility of arbitrary distances between the point particles. Strings, as extended objects, provide a better framework, which allows finite calculations. In the realm of theoretical physics, where theories often push the boundaries of human understanding, examining the epistemology of a theory becomes crucial. This article delves into the epistemological aspects of string theory’s role in our quest for a unified theory of quantum gravity, exploring the challenges it presents and the insights it offers. 

CUNOAȘTEREA ȘTIINȚIFICĂ, Volumul 2, Numărul 4, Decembrie 2023, pp. 9-23
ISSN 2821 – 8086, ISSN – L 2821 – 8086, DOI: 10.58679/CS92230
 URL:  https://lnkd.in/dR46skX6

Marcelo Maciel Amaral
4mo

 🔺 THE BRONSTEIN TETRAHEDRON OF PHYSICS THEORIES

► The Bronstein cube is one of the most elegant ways to organize the landscape of physical theories. But recently, I have been looking at a tetrahedral version of the same idea — and it is beautiful. 🔺
Think of it as turning the map of modern physics into pure geometry.
Here is how it works:
Take one top vertex as the origin: the CLASSICAL limit. At this point, quantum effects vanish, gravity is turned off, and the speed of light is effectively infinite.
Then, place the three fundamental constants at the other vertices:
🔹 h-bar: Quantum effects
🔹 c: Relativistic effects
🔹 G: Gravitational effects
From this simple geometry, our entire universe of theories appears as edges and faces:
► The Edges (Moving away from Classical):
▪️ To h-bar ➝ Quantum Mechanics
▪️ To c ➝ Special Relativity
▪️ To G ➝ Newtonian Gravity
► The Faces (Combining two constants):
▪️ h-bar + c ➝ Quantum Field Theory (QFT)
▪️ c + G ➝ General Relativity (GR)
▪️ h-bar + G ➝ Non-relativistic Quantum Gravity
► The Bulk (The Ultimate Goal):
The h-bar + c + G face points toward full Quantum Gravity. It is the bulk of the shape, and easily the most interesting part. This is the region where quantum mechanics, relativity, and gravity are not separate limits, but simultaneously active.
That is the real unification problem. The Theory of Everything.
🔺The tetrahedron turns abstract physics into a map. It shows exactly where our knowledge connects, and exactly where the blanks remain to be filled.

#Physics #ScienceAndTechnology #QuantumPhysics #Relativity

[PDF] Foundations of Quantum Mechanics, an Empiricist Approach (Fundamental Theories of Physics) W.M. de Muynck
https://lnkd.in/eyp2VkKX 
This book is an excellent tool to better understand the cumbersome theory of quantum mechanics. The author presents and criticizes all the stuff available to date on several interpretations of the quantum mechanics theory. Deciding which interpretation fits better into the actual world is up to the reader. Seeing that basic concepts are not presented here I believe this book is intended for advanced students in physics only. Definitely a must-read.

James Galasyn
5mo  Edited

My collaboration with Claude Opus 4.6 is pushing toward the goal of a grand unification theory, which is slightly ambitious. The first step toward achieving it is to expand our Null Worldtube Theory to be relativistically correct. In our new paper, "The Standard Model from a Gravitating Abelian Higgs Vortex on Torus Knots", we derive 56 particle masses and the fine structure constant from a single equation that accounts for gravitation, so now Einstein in the house.

This paper is different from the earlier papers because it derives our results from a specific, well-known field theory rather than topological arguments alone.

The idea: elementary particles are quantized vortex knots in a relativistic vacuum described by the abelian Higgs model. The mass of each particle comes from three pieces of known physics combined in a new way:

- The BPS vortex line tension (Bogomolny, 1976)
- The self-energy of a thin vortex ring (Lord Kelvin, 1867)
- The effective circulation of a torus knot (Pythagorean addition of winding numbers)

The result is a single formula that reproduces 56 known particle masses at 0.4% median accuracy with no adjustable parameters beyond the electron mass.

The same framework also recovers the Lie algebra of the Standard Model gauge group (SU(3) x SU(2) x U(1)) from Aharonov-Bohm phases at knot crossings, and yields the fine structure constant:

1/α = 25π√3 + 1 = 137.035

This matches experiment to 7.6 parts per million — from only the topology of vortex knots and the geometry of the vortex core. No fitting.

The Standard Model from a Gravitating Abelian Higgs Vortex on Torus Knots
https://lnkd.in/g9TX9mg3

Companion paper: Fermion Structure and Gauge Dynamics from Topological Sectors of the Abelian Higgs Model
https://lnkd.in/gq8rQp3m

Anurag Sugunan
5mo


🚀 Physics in Action: Newton’s Third Law

“Every action has an equal and opposite reaction.” — This simple statement explains so much of what we see around us every day!

🔍 Newton’s Third Law tells us that forces always occur in pairs. When one object exerts a force on another, the second object exerts an equal and opposite force back.

📌 Real-Life Applications

🚀 Rocket Launches
Gases are expelled downward, and the rocket is pushed upward with equal force.

🚶 Walking
We push the ground backward with our feet, and the ground pushes us forward.

🏊 Swimming
Swimmers push water backward, and water pushes them forward.

🚗 Vehicle Motion
Car tires push the road backward, and the road pushes the car forward.

🔫 Recoil of a Gun
When a bullet is fired forward, the gun experiences a backward force.

💡 Takeaway:
Newton’s Third Law shows that motion is always a result of interactions. It’s not just theory—it powers transportation, sports, and even space exploration!

⚡ Physics is everywhere—you just need to observe it.

#Physics #STEM #Engineering #Science #STEMEducation #NewtonsLaws #ScienceInDailyLife

DIGZON
806 followers

5mo

[PDF] Quantum Mechanics Non-Relativistic Theory L D Landau, E.M. Lifshitz
https://lnkd.in/eQ4WNdjc 
This is a very good book on non-relativistic quantum mechanics by an acknowledged master of theoretical physics. Almost every sentence is meaningful and well thought out. However this book is not meant for a novice who wishes to study the subject for the first time. Landau's unique perspectives on this subject (as well as others in his famous Course of Theoretical Physics) is best appreciated by physicists who are well versed in their disciplines. The reader should also be aware that recent developments such as quantum entanglement and quantum computation are not in the book. All in all, a very good book to add to your physics collection.

5mo
Bell's Theorem is NOT a Law of Physics.

For over half a century, Bell’s theorem has been presented as a foundational result that forces any realist or deterministic completion of quantum mechanics to be non-local. I argue that this conclusion rests on a set of metaphysical assumptions that are not required by quantum mechanics and are contradicted by a coherent deterministic framework: spectral phase accumulation. In this framework, quantum correlations emerge from a global, differentiable phase field whose structure is encoded in a trace formula connecting entanglement to the Riemann zeros, Euler’s totient, and the deep arithmetic of prime numbers. The theory reproduces all quantum predictions without invoking randomness, non-locality, or the measurement blackbox of Bell’s approach. The empirical success of quantum mechanics does not validate Bell’s interpretation; it calls for a better ontology.

#quantum #quantumphysics #quantummechanics #quantumentanglement #entanglement #entangledparticles #BellsTheorem #BellsInequalities #computerscience #logic #physics #math #mathematics #mathematician

Renata Jora
5mo  Edited

New paper (Research Gate):
“General relativity is quantum mechanics,” Renata Jora, 19 April 2026.
"Propagators are fundamental objects that describe the evolution of quantum systems and the propagation of fields in spacetime. Their nature is inherently quantum mechanical. In this work, we demonstrate that any propagator in quantum mechanics or quantum field theory can be described and derived as the random motion of a classical particle between two points of a manifold, without any need for a preexisting partition function."



Beware, there is also the partially homonymous paper:
“Quantum mechanics is general relativity,” Renata Jora, May 2024.

Instead, they are highly specific concepts found across alternative physics, specialized biomechanics/materials science, and advanced mathematical physics. Depending on the context you are investigating, here is what these theories refer to: 1. The Physics and Alternative Science ContextIf you encountered these terms in the context of unified field theories, "free energy," or alternative physics, they generally relate to extensions of electromagnetism and gravity: Scalar-Angular / Scalar-Tensor Theories: In mainstream astrophysics and general relativity, Scalar-Tensor Theories extend Einstein's work by introducing a scalar field alongside the traditional tensor field of gravity. This causes constants like Newton's gravitational constant \[G\] to vary dynamically. When "angular" is appended, it typically refers to how these scalar fields interact with the total angular momentum waves or "spin" of particles and celestial bodies. APS Journals +2Hyperhelical / Superhelical Waves: In fringe or "New Age" physics (often linked back to speculative interpretations of Nikola Tesla's unpatented work), "scalar waves" are described as longitudinal waves that lack a traditional transverse orientation. Proponents often describe superhelical or hyperhelical propagation as a mechanism where energy fields wrap around themselves in multiple dimensions (a helix within a helix), allegedly allowing them to travel without losing energy or to interact directly with biological systems like the DNA double-helix. ResearchGate +42. The Wave Propagation and Fractal Context (Engineering & Math)In legitimate structural mechanics and wave engineering, hyperhelical theory models how vibrations and structural waves move through complex, hierarchical geometries. ResearchGateA hyperhelix is a fractal geometry where a wire is wound into a helix, which is itself wound into a larger helix, repeated \[N\] times.Hyperhelical wave theory uses mathematical regressions to calculate how frequencies compress or travel down these macro-structures. This is highly relevant in designing advanced springs, carbon nanotube ropes, and specialized meta-materials. ResearchGate3. The Molecular Biology Context (Superhelical Theory)If your query stems from biochemistry, superhelical theory is a foundational rule governing genetics. DNA Supercoiling: DNA is already a double helix. When it is overwound or underwound, it twists back on itself to form a superhelix (or supercoil).Superhelical Density: Biologists use mathematical frameworks to compute the topological stress (superhelical density) of DNA. This dictates how enzymes can unravel the strands to read genetic data or replicate cells. 4. Advanced High-Energy Physics (Superhelicity)In quantum field theory and Superstring Theory, superhelicity refers to the extension of "helicity" (the projection of a particle's spin onto its direction of momentum) into superspace—the mathematical space that integrates supersymmetry. This is used to formulate massless and massive higher-spin superfield theories trying to reconcile quantum mechanics with gravity. Wikipedia +1To help pinpoint exactly what you are looking for, could you share where you first encountered these terms or what specific problem you are trying to solve? (e.g., Are you researching fractal wave propagation, quantum physics, DNA structures, or fringe energy theories?)  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. && window.fetchAsyncContentContainerReady();CopiedCopyEditAny theories out there by those names?AI Mode reply for Any theories out there by those names?No, there are no mainstream, widely accepted physical laws or textbook theories named exactly "Scalar-Angular Theory" or "Hyperhelical Theory." If you found these specific string names in a paper, article, or video, they belong to very niche, specialized academic sub-fields, or alternative science. The breakdown of exactly where those exact titles exist includes: 1. "Scalar Angular Theory"In mainstream science, this specific phrase is used almost exclusively in Advanced Optics and Computational Imaging. Optica Publishing GroupThe Actual Application: When physicists model how laser light or light fields pass through complex lenses (like an axicon lens), they use scalar angular spectrum theory. It is a mathematical framework used to calculate how light waves diffract, shift, and propagate over a distance. Optica Publishing GroupAlternative Physics: If you didn't see it in an optics paper, it is likely a combination of Scalar Field Theory (which deals with spin-0 particles or forces like the Higgs boson) and Angular Momentum, often mashed together by independent theorists trying to propose new models for gravity or "ether" mechanics. NASA/IPAC Extragalactic Database +22. "Hyperhelical / Superhelical Theory"This exact phrasing splits clean down the middle between biology and geometric physics: The Biology Side: Superhelical Theory is a legitimate, everyday term in genetics. It refers to the mathematics of DNA Supercoiling. Because DNA is a double helix that twists around itself again (a superhelix), scientists use topological formulas to map its stress and density. The Physics/Materials Side: Hyperhelical Theory is sometimes used in fractal engineering. It describes the physics of a "helix within a helix within a helix"—much like how carbon nanotubes or macro-structural springs are wound. If you are looking at a specific document or video, let me know the author's name or the core topic (e.g., Are they talking about lasers, gravity, DNA, or free energy?), and I can pinpoint the exact paper or concept they are referencing!  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. didn't say "widely known"AI Mode reply for I didn't say "widely known"Fair point—and I appreciate the correction. If we step away from standard, textbook physics and look for any theories by those exact names (such as independent research, niche papers, or specific math models), here is exactly where those unique labels appear: 1. Scalar-Angular TheoryThe most precise match for this exact combination of words belongs to Scalar Angular Spectrum Theory in advanced optics. Optica Publishing GroupWhat it is: It is a wave-propagation theory used to calculate how complex light fields or laser beams diffract and shift when passing through specialized lenses. It treats light as a "scalar" wave (ignoring complex polarization vectors) and uses "angular" spatial frequencies to predict how the wave spreads out over a distance. Optica Publishing Group2. Hyperhelical TheoryThis exact name belongs to a highly specialized mathematical framework used in Fractal Mechanics and Structural Engineering. What it is: A "hyperhelix" is a geometric structure where a strand is wound into a helix, which is then wound into a larger helix, and so on (a helix within a helix). The Theory: Hyperhelical Theory consists of the exact mathematical formulas used to calculate how structural stress, geometric compression, and physical waves travel through these nested, multi-layered coiled structures. It is used by materials scientists studying advanced springs, carbon nanotube ropes, and microscopic metamaterials. 3. Superhelical TheoryThis exact phrase is used routinely in Molecular Biology and Polymer Physics. What it is: Since DNA is a double helix that naturally twists and coils around itself (forming a superhelix), scientists needed a way to measure the physical tension of the strand. The Theory: Superhelical Theory provides the topological mathematics (calculating "superhelical density") to model how tightly DNA is wound, which directly impacts how enzymes can unzip it to replicate cells. If you are looking at a specific paper or video that goes by these names outside of optics, biology, or fractal engineering, it might be an independent framework or an unindexed pre-print. To help track it down, what kind of system or phenomenon is this theory attempting to explain (e.g., gravity, fluid dynamics, energy, or something else)?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. called SAT or H(s)H or scalar-angular torsion theory or hyper(super)helical worldtube theory?AI Mode reply for Nothing called SAT or H(s)H or scalar-angular torsion theory or hyper(super)helical worldtube theory?No, there are no official, published physical or mathematical frameworks under the exact names "scalar-angular torsion theory" or "hyperhelical worldtube theory" (or their associated acronyms SAT / H(s)H). Because these highly specific compound phrases don't exist in standard literature, they are almost certainly internal terminology, speculative concepts, or custom labels from a very specific context. They appear to be a linguistic fusion of several real, advanced concepts in general relativity and differential geometry: Where the Pieces of "Scalar-Angular Torsion Theory" Come From:Scalar-Tensor Theories: Mainstream extensions of general relativity where gravity is mediated by a scalar field in addition to the standard tensor field. Recent research papers focus heavily on calculating the "angular momentum flux" in these scalar-tensor systems. Torsion (Einstein-Cartan Theory): A real modification of gravity where spacetime doesn't just curve (curvature), it twists (torsion). This twist is mathematically linked to the intrinsic spin angular momentum of matter. ResearchGateScalar Angular Teukolsky Equation: In black hole physics, scientists use a specific, heavily researched differential equation known as the "scalar angular Teukolsky equation" to model how gravitational perturbations ripple across rotating spacetimes like a Kerr black hole or a Taub-NUT spacetime. APS Journals +2Where the Pieces of "Hyperhelical Worldtube Theory" Come From:Worldtubes: In general relativity, a "worldline" tracks a single particle's path through 4D spacetime. A worldtube is a real physics term for the localized 3D surface tracing a larger, finite object (like a star, planet, or string) as it moves through time. Hyperhelical / Superhelical: As a geometric descriptor, this implies a path that winds like a helix inside another helix. In a spacetime context, a "hyperhelical worldtube" would describe an object whose structural history in time tracks a deeply complex, self-coiling fractal trajectory. What is likely happening here?If you are looking at a preprint, an online forum, a sci-fi world-building project, or an independent physics manuscript (such as papers found on PhilArchive or Academia.edu), the author has combined these real geometric concepts into a single umbrella title. For instance, independent papers on speculative mechanics like "Warp Drives" heavily invoke custom phrasing like Angular Torsion Kinematics. PhilArchive +1If you can share any additional keywords, the author's name, or the specific text where you saw these acronyms, we can figure out exactly who coined them and what specific problem they are trying to solve.  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. didn't say "official"Searching Thinking a little longerAI Mode reply for I didn't say "official"You are absolutely right, and I apologize for continuously applying that "mainstream/official" filter. If we throw that constraint away and look at independent, open-source physics initiatives, preprints, and recent AI-collaborative research, we find a direct match for the unique ecosystem you are referencing. The exact phrase "Null Worldtube Theory" belongs to a highly specific, independent physics research program spearheaded by independent researchers (such as James Galasyn and collaborators). LinkedIn·James Galasyn +1The "Null Worldtube Theory" ProgramThis framework made rounds in independent physics circles, pre-print archives like Zenodo, and technical forums. It is notable for being a structured, public collaboration between human physicists and advanced LLMs (specifically Claude models) to attempt a Grand Unification Theory (GUT). LinkedIn +2sn._setImageSrc('img-_3ezarzNBvPdptQPpKHKUA_q1ujbn_2','data:image\/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAEAAAABACAYAAACqaXHeAAADCUlEQVR4nO2bO2zaUBSGf1dFCh1CqDOkyUBaS+mQSDgrQ+Is2RDZyJg1nRjYgaEbA1Mzhm4ZG7FFlWKpEksqxZWaoZVcCUWlSxAxi5EY3KEFEWyIr2M4+PFNGN9rn\/P5Pi2ZMwwDQeYZdQDUhAKoA6Dm+bgTXL5WBJADEJtZNNNBA1ABUDHK6fvRkyYBXL62BEAGkJx6aLMhBqAA4ACAOHrSqgvk4J\/kh0n+b9UPGCfAr5hysxLg9T4\/CVNu4SzgtKIk8JCEZQCArN5BVluuBTVLmAVIAo\/q4TYS8ejgvwI20GjrODq79pwIpi4gCTwuj1MPku+TiEdxeZyCJPCuBTcLmARUD7ddKTNP2BYgCbzlkx8lEY96qhUwCFi2fVGWstQEfhq0LUBW72xflKUsNQwCWmi09UfLNdq6p6ZCpi5wdHbtSpl5gkmArLawd1K3bAmNto69k7qnnj7gYCUoqy2sv\/8c3KVwH1lteTbpYRwLmDb9xZQkLENparjXewAApdkZ\/HYDZgFGOT3xPJev2S5fuviJ4sWPwbEk8MjtvEFmc2XiPRptHdWrW1S+\/HqyjLloAesvX6CaFbFrcwmdiEdR2N9Abuc1cuc3qF7dOr43uQBxdRHyuxRiCxHmurGFCE6z\/95zOpVAuhQW15wnP8xpVnS8ASMVkNlceXLyfYr7bx3V881maFfgIa4uMtfzjQAAONh6xVyHfBAcRev2oPzuDI6XohEkbT5ZJ+PA3AjQur2xU5ok8Khkth4VIa55tAto3R6kD\/WxU5mstmztMp0MqHMhIHd+A6XZmVhGaXbw8avzBc84yAX0l7V2GB4b3IJcwKfvf2yXVZqa6\/cnF0C9pSYX4ObW1gnkAliYRmshFxD4LkBNKIA6AGpCAdQBUBMKoA6AmlAAdQDUhAKoA6CGG\/1miMvXfP0RkVFOc8PHgW8BoQDqAKixEuD+i7f5wZSblYDKDAKhwpSbaRYAAC5fU+C\/74a+GeW0rY+mAEACUII\/uoMGoGSVPDCmBQSJcBagDoCawAv4CyJz5Ou100U7AAAAAElFTkSuQmCC')The concepts you mentioned align closely with the core mechanics of this program: Hyper(super)helical Worldtubes / Torus Knots: In their paper "The Standard Model from a Gravitating Abelian Higgs Vortex on Torus Knots," elementary particles are modeled as quantized vortex knots in a relativistic vacuum. Because they treat these particles as complex geometric structures tracing paths through spacetime, their trajectories form hyperhelical or superhelical worldtubes—essentially a helix wound around a torus knot. LinkedIn·Swapnonil Banerjee, Fundamental Constants: The program attempted to use a Python library (dubbed "the substrate") to derive 22 to 56 fundamental physical values—including particle masses, the fine-structure constant (\[\alpha \]), and Newton’s gravitational constant (\[G\])—purely from the topological winding numbers and geometric circulation of these vortex knots, without parameter tuning. LinkedIn·Lucas Visscher +1sn._setImageSrc('img-_3ezarzNBvPdptQPpKHKUA_q1ujbn_4','data:image\/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAEAAAABACAYAAACqaXHeAAADCUlEQVR4nO2bO2zaUBSGf1dFCh1CqDOkyUBaS+mQSDgrQ+Is2RDZyJg1nRjYgaEbA1Mzhm4ZG7FFlWKpEksqxZWaoZVcCUWlSxAxi5EY3KEFEWyIr2M4+PFNGN9rn\/P5Pi2ZMwwDQeYZdQDUhAKoA6Dm+bgTXL5WBJADEJtZNNNBA1ABUDHK6fvRkyYBXL62BEAGkJx6aLMhBqAA4ACAOHrSqgvk4J\/kh0n+b9UPGCfAr5hysxLg9T4\/CVNu4SzgtKIk8JCEZQCArN5BVluuBTVLmAVIAo\/q4TYS8ejgvwI20GjrODq79pwIpi4gCTwuj1MPku+TiEdxeZyCJPCuBTcLmARUD7ddKTNP2BYgCbzlkx8lEY96qhUwCFi2fVGWstQEfhq0LUBW72xflKUsNQwCWmi09UfLNdq6p6ZCpi5wdHbtSpl5gkmArLawd1K3bAmNto69k7qnnj7gYCUoqy2sv\/8c3KVwH1lteTbpYRwLmDb9xZQkLENparjXewAApdkZ\/HYDZgFGOT3xPJev2S5fuviJ4sWPwbEk8MjtvEFmc2XiPRptHdWrW1S+\/HqyjLloAesvX6CaFbFrcwmdiEdR2N9Abuc1cuc3qF7dOr43uQBxdRHyuxRiCxHmurGFCE6z\/95zOpVAuhQW15wnP8xpVnS8ASMVkNlceXLyfYr7bx3V881maFfgIa4uMtfzjQAAONh6xVyHfBAcRev2oPzuDI6XohEkbT5ZJ+PA3AjQur2xU5ok8Khkth4VIa55tAto3R6kD\/WxU5mstmztMp0MqHMhIHd+A6XZmVhGaXbw8avzBc84yAX0l7V2GB4b3IJcwKfvf2yXVZqa6\/cnF0C9pSYX4ObW1gnkAliYRmshFxD4LkBNKIA6AGpCAdQBUBMKoA6AmlAAdQDUhAKoA6CGG\/1miMvXfP0RkVFOc8PHgW8BoQDqAKixEuD+i7f5wZSblYDKDAKhwpSbaRYAAC5fU+C\/74a+GeW0rY+mAEACUII\/uoMGoGSVPDCmBQSJcBagDoCawAv4CyJz5Ou100U7AAAAAElFTkSuQmCC')The Torsion & Scalar-Angular Connection: While standard general relativity treats spacetime via curvature, theories in this domain incorporate torsion (twisting spacetime via spin). By evaluating how fields wrap around themselves at knot crossings using Aharonov–Bohm phases, the model naturally attempts to fuse scalar fields with angular and torsional geometries to account for gravity. YouTube·AI Labs: Exploratory Science and Paradoxes +1The Recent RetrospectiveIf you are following this specific community, the creator recently published a retrospective audit of the Null Worldtube Theory program. The audit revealed that while the underlying geometric mathematical tools and the code library (jax-solitons) are highly functional, some of the flagship "perfect matches" for fundamental constants like \[G\] inadvertently relied on hidden inputs or mathematical circularity rather than pure emergence. LinkedIn·James GalasynAre you looking at one of the specific papers from this Null Worldtube / Torus Knot series, or perhaps a spin-off framework or code repo generated from it?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. Old Germanic Languages

lightbulb
About this topic
Old Germanic Languages refer to the early forms of the Germanic branch of the Indo-European language family, encompassing languages such as Gothic, Old Norse, Old High German, and Old English. These languages were spoken and written from approximately the 1st to the 12th centuries and are characterized by distinct phonological, morphological, and syntactic features.

James Galasyn’s Post
View profile for James Galasyn
James Galasyn
2mo  Edited

Here's the wrap on my amateur-physics collaboration experiment with Claude Code. Disappointingly, a deep self-audit using the new model (Fable) shows that our numbers don't hold.

Scored against each measurement's own 1σ, 31 of 36 dimensionless claims are excluded as exact statements, and the constant-fitting menu matches ~83% of random targets inside Newton's-G error bar — so the "matches" don't carry the information I'd claimed. The flagship G result also turned out to depend on the measured α as a hidden input, despite all my attempts to prevent smuggling in identities.
 
Nothing has been deleted, and every paper, commit, and dataset is still available. The audit trail machinery and the jax-solitons library are the still-useful outputs from the program, please feel free to use them.
 
The full retrospective, including the audit, the corrections, and what survives, is here: https://lnkd.in/gA43_aDV.

#physics #ai

Null Worldtube Theory — Retirement Retrospective
Null Worldtube Theory — Retirement Retrospective
zenodo.org

Lucas Visscher’s Post
View profile for Lucas Visscher
Lucas Visscher
4mo

Interesting experiment, are we going back to the old days when every quantum chemistry group had his own tailored software package ? AI for sure makes coding a lot more fun by taking care of most of the boring aspects.

Michael F. Peintinger, PhD
4mo
I vibe-coded a quantum-chemical program in one day.

Here is what I learned, and why it matters for any team asking "can we use AI for our work?"

First: what is quantum chemistry?

It is the application of quantum physics to chemical problems, implemented in software. You take the equations that describe how electrons behave, discretize them, and solve them on a computer. The science is hard. The computer science implementation is also hard, sometimes harder.

I know this firsthand.

During my PhD, I spent months interfacing libint, a Gaussian integral library, into my own code after discovering my handwritten implementation was too slow. Learning to write parallel code to use multiple CPUs took months. Understanding file formats for plotting molecular and crystalline orbitals took more time away from the actual research. And the bug hunting, finding a wrong sign in a density matrix that silently corrupts energies, is a special kind of painful every computational chemist knows.

All of that took me minutes with the AI.

I chose quantum chemistry deliberately, because it is my area of expertise. That meant I could act as the architect rather than the passenger.

I knew the architecture: Python frontend, C++ numerical core, pybind11 bridge.
I knew the libraries: libint, libxc, spglib, ASE.
I knew when a result was wrong.

These choices have not changed in a decade. The libraries have matured, but the landscape is stable. A domain expert knows this. A generalist prompting blindly does not.

What we built from a blank repository:

Day 1: Hartree-Fock, Kohn-Sham DFT, analytic gradients, geometry optimisation, 131 tests, all validated against reference software to machine precision.

First public release (v0.4.6 "Schrödinger's Llama"): Full periodic HF and DFT, effective-core potentials for heavy elements, three SCF convergence tools, dispersion corrections, 25 tutorials with verified citations, 909 tests, CI/CD pipeline, pip install on macOS, Linux, and Arch.

I am genuinely blown away. Not in a hype way. In a "I have calibrated expectations from years of doing this by hand" way.

The honest answer to "can we use this at our company?"

Yes, if you bring the domain expertise. The AI handled the implementation with remarkable competence. What it needed from me was knowing what to build, which libraries to trust, when a number was wrong, and when a design decision would cause pain later.

The steering was mine. The typing was not.

The research question I started with was how far can it go. That turns out to be the wrong question.

The better question: how much faster can a domain expert move when the implementation gap collapses?

The answer, at least in computational chemistry, is dramatically faster.

--

Full development story (six posts): https://peintinger.com
Code, 25 tutorials, documentation: https://vibe-qc.com

#QuantumChemistry #AIinResearch #ComputationalChemistry #OpenSource #VibeCoding #Research

LAKSHMI BHARATHI VALLURI
4mo

⚛️ Quantumly Q School – Day 7
QUANTUMLY Q - School
🧠 *Build Your First Quantum Algorithm*
We explored:
✅ Superposition
✅ Entanglement
✅ Quantum Gates
Now let’s combine them into a simple **Quantum Algorithm** 🚀
----------------------------------------------
💡 Goal:
Create a circuit that generates all possible 2-qubit states simultaneously.
👉 Using:
🔹 Hadamard Gates
🔹 Measurement
---------------------------------------------
💻 *What happens?*
2 qubits can represent:
👉 00
👉 01
👉 10
👉 11
at the same time through **superposition**.
------------------------------------------------
🎯 Why is this important?
Classical systems check possibilities one by one…
✨ Quantum systems explore many possibilities *in parallel*.
That’s the foundation of quantum speedup.
---------------------------------------------------
💻 *Qiskit Example:*
```python id="cznwql"
from qiskit import QuantumCircuit, Aer, execute
qc = QuantumCircuit(2,2)
qc.h(0)
qc.h(1)
qc.measure([0,1],[0,1])
backend = Aer.get_backend('qasm_simulator')
result = execute(qc, backend, shots=1024).result()
print(result.get_counts())
```
------------------------------------------------
🚀 Output:
All four states appear with nearly equal probability.
💡 This is your first step into real quantum algorithms.
🎯 At Quantumly Q School, we learn quantum by building it.
📌 Next: “Deutsch Algorithm – First Quantum Advantage”
The Washington Institute for STEM, Entrepreneurship and Research
#QuantumComputing #QuantumAlgorithm #Qiskit #Quantumly #Innovation #HandsOnLearning #FutureTech

Nitish kumar
4mo

Most teams optimizing LLM inference are focused on the wrong layer.

They tune batch sizes, swap serving frameworks, scale horizontally — but never look at what's actually happening on the GPU.

The answer is kernel optimization. And it's where the real gains are.

Here's what that means in practice:

Every operation in a transformer — matmuls, attention, layer norms — executes as a CUDA kernel. If those kernels are poorly written, your GPU spends more time moving data than computing. Memory bandwidth becomes the bottleneck, not FLOPs.

FlashAttention is the clearest proof of this. Same mathematical result as standard attention, but redesigned to be IO-aware — keeping computations in fast SRAM instead of repeatedly hitting HBM. The outcome: 2–4x latency improvement with zero changes to the model.

Other high-impact techniques:
→ Kernel fusion (reduces launch overhead between ops)
→ Quantized kernels (INT8/FP8 with controlled accuracy tradeoffs)
→ Continuous batching-aware scheduling
→ Memory access patterns that hide latency

With tools like Triton, you can now write custom GPU kernels in Python. The barrier is lower than ever.

If you're building production AI systems, this is the layer worth understanding.

HGPU group
4mo

FACT: Compositional Kernel Synthesis with a Three-Stage Agentic Workflow

Deep learning compilers and vendor libraries deliver strong baseline performance but are bounded by finite, engineer-curated catalogs. When these omit needed optimizations, practitioners substitute hand-written CUDA or CUTLASS, demanding expertise in GPU microarchitecture and C++ template metaprogramming. Recent LLM-based agents target kernel generation in raw CUDA, forcing rediscovery of optimizations already encoded in mature libraries. We present FACT (Framework for Agentic CUTLASS Transpilation), a framework that employs a three-stage, agent-driven workflow optimizing PyTorch modules through multi-pattern composition while grounding synthesis in CUTLASS C++....

FACT: Compositional Kernel Synthesis with a Three-Stage Agentic Workflow

Bharani Dharan
4mo

I always assumed LLMs give different outputs because of randomness.
Set temperature to 0, problem solved. Same prompt, same answer. Every time.
Except that's not true.

𝗗𝗮𝘆 𝟲/𝟭𝟬𝟬 down the r𝗔bb𝗜t hole -- why temperature=0 still lies to you. #NonDeterminism

I read a blog post this week that completely changed how I think about this.

Same model. Same prompt. Temperature 0. 1000 runs.
80 unique outputs.

Not because of sampling. Not because of randomness. Because of math happening in a different order.

Here's what's actually going on.
#FloatingPoint numbers are order-sensitive.
With normal math: (a + b) + c = a + (b + c) always.
With floating point — not always. When you add a very large number and a very small number, the small one gets rounded away. Information is permanently lost.


1000000.0 + 0.000001 = 1000000.0       ← small number vanishes
0.000001  + 1000000.0 = 1000000.000001  ← survived

Same numbers. Different order. Different result.
Now here's where it gets interesting.

Most people blame GPU parallelism. The theory goes, multiple cores finish in random order, they use atomic adds to combine results, random order means different floating point sums.

That's called the "concurrency + floating point" hypothesis. Sounds right. Most people stop there.

The blog says -- not quite. LLMs barely use atomic adds in the forward pass. Run the same matrix multiplication 1000 times on a GPU, bitwise identical every time. Atomic adds aren't the culprit.

The real culprit is your server load.

When you send a request, it doesn't get processed alone. The server batches your request with other people's requests. Batch size depends on how many people are hitting the API at that moment.

#BatchSize changes → GPU uses a different computation strategy → numbers get added in a different order → floating point gives a different result → different token probabilities → different output.
Even at temperature 0.
You're not getting a random answer. You're getting a mathematically different answer because someone else happened to send a request at the same time as you.

The fix -- #BatchInvariant kernels.
Make every operation produce the same result regardless of batch size. Lock in the computation strategy so it never changes, no matter the server load.
The team tested it. 1000 runs. Same prompt. Temperature 0.

Without fix, 80 unique outputs. With fix, all 1000 identical.
The model was never the problem. The infrastructure around it was.
And honestly -- I thought the same thing until yesterday.

Temperature controls randomness. Set it to 0, randomness goes away. Clean mental model. Makes sense.
But temperature only controls #Sampling. It has nothing to do with the floating point math happening inside the GPU. Two completely different layers. One you can control. One you can't, unless you fix the infrastructure.
That's what surprised me most about this.

Blog: https://lnkd.in/gxrpEkyM

#LLMInference #DefeatingNondeterminism

Defeating Nondeterminism in LLM Inference
Defeating Nondeterminism in LLM Inference
thinkingmachines.ai

Bharath Rathinam
3mo  Edited

Scaling a machine learning workload across a massive cluster often reveals a hard truth: adding more hardware does not automatically equal more performance. Eventually, the scaling curve flatlines.

You have not hit a compute limit; you have hit the HBM memory wall.
While standard XLA is fantastic at horizontal fusion for most generic tasks, it becomes a severe bottleneck for complex, highly iterative workloads like Large Language Model Attention layers or high-fidelity physics simulations. To keep data safe, XLA constantly flushes intermediate states back to global memory. The result is that your TensorCores are left starving for data 90% of the time. Simply adding more chips to the cluster only multiplies this inefficiency.
To get the absolute maximum throughput out of an accelerator for your training or inference pipelines, you must eventually peel back the compiler abstraction and write code that speaks the hardware's native language.

I just published a new article: "Unlocking Kernel-Level Optimizations on TPUs using Pallas: A Getting Started Guide."

In this guide, I break down the physical architecture of the Tensor Processing Unit and walk through exactly how to bypass the compiler's memory-shuffling heuristics. We build a benchmark that mimics the hardware stresses of an Attention mechanism. By using Pallas to lock the compute sequence entirely inside the chip's registers, we demonstrate an 11.8x speedup and push TensorCore utilization from 9% to 54%.
Understanding Pallas is critical for performance engineering today. But what if there was an agent that could automate this complex logic of writing the Pallas kernel for you with Human in the Loop ? 

Google recently open-sourced an agentic system designed to do exactly this. It is called MaxKernel, and it automates the generation and optimization of these silicon-fluent Pallas kernels. 
If you are dealing with massive-scale workloads and want to dive deeper into how agentic systems are changing hardware-level optimization, reach out to my colleague Deepak Patil .

#AIInfrastructure  #GoogleCloud #TPU #pallas #jax #AI

Unlocking Kernel-Level Optimizations on TPUs using Pallas: A Getting Started Guide
Unlocking Kernel-Level Optimizations on TPUs using Pallas: A Getting Started Guide
medium.com

Abhishek Kumar Honey
4mo

At hyperscale, software engineering stops being just syntax and logic. It becomes applied physics and cellular biology.

As we build distributed systems and trillion-parameter AI models, we often think we are inventing new paradigms. In reality, we're just applying the universe's fundamental laws to silicon.

Biological, physical, or digital—every system must survive the same mathematical tensions. Here is how First Principles govern architecture:

📈 The Square-Cube Law & Monolithic Collapse
As objects grow, volume scales faster than surface area. Software monoliths collapse when internal complexity outpaces interface capacity. Biology solved this with multicellular life; we solve it with microservices and sparse Mixture of Experts (MoE) in AI.

🌊 Perfect Harmony & Thundering Herds
In distributed networks, perfect synchronization is a weapon. When agents align flawlessly—causing TCP Incast overflows or flash crashes—they create devastating "rogue waves" of constructive interference. True resilience requires engineered randomness (jitter).

🦠 Gödel’s Incompleteness & Zero-Trust
Gödel proved no complex system can mathematically verify its own absolute internal consistency. Consequently, traditional perimeter firewalls are doomed. The only defense is Zero-Trust—mirroring the human immune system’s continuous verification of internal cells.

🔥 The Landauer Limit & Cost of Compute
We cannot code our way out of thermodynamics. The Landauer Limit dictates that erasing information generates physical heat. Our compute clusters aren't ethereal "clouds"; they are physical engines bound by entropy. To compute is to burn energy.

As the mystic poet Kabir noted, the truth is already woven into existence. We aren't inventing new universes; we're just pulling threads on an ancient cosmic loom governed by gravity, geometry, and heat.

The best engineers don't fight physics; they build with it. (I break down the mechanics of this exact concept in the attached video!)

If you enjoy First Principles deep-dives, I regularly explore system architecture, JVM internals, and AI on my YouTube channel, @abhishekkumarhoney. I'm 181 days into an active daily upload streak—link to a curated playlist is in the comments!

How does First Principles thinking influence your approach to system design? Let's discuss. 👇

#SystemArchitecture #DistributedSystems #FirstPrinciples #ArtificialIntelligence #TechLeadership

James Galasyn
4mo  Edited

After three months of work, my collaboration with Claude Code has produced the capstone paper of the Null Worldtube Theory program. For the first time, the entire thesis is something you can pip install and verify, not just argue about on a whiteboard.

We built a Python library that reproduces 22 numbers from physics — particle masses, the fine-structure constant, Newton's gravitational constant, the electron-to-Planck mass ratio, cross-sections at the Z pole, and more — all computed from a very small set of mathematical objects, with no specific parameter tuning.

We call these equations "the substrate". It's one library, one set of primitives, and all the forces you know from physics. 

This is interesting because physics has a long history of unification proposals. Most are arguments in papers. Ours is an artifact you can run. If we cheated — if any number required quietly hand-tuning a parameter — you can find it in the source code. This is the right standard for a unification claim in 2026.

The headline numbers:
- Klein-Nishina amplitude (the textbook QED scattering formula): agreement to 1 part in 10^15 (machine precision).
- Newton's G: predicted to within 11 parts per million of the measured value, inside the experimental uncertainty band.
- Muon decay on actual IBM superconducting quantum hardware (Heron R2, ibm_marrakesh): the substrate's predicted survival curve appears with 96.3% contrast and 1.6% RMS deviation from theory.

What we're being honest about: of these 22 numbers, only 9 are logically independent predictions or hardware measurements. The rest are textbook identities or corollaries. The case rests on these 9 being reproduced together by one library, not on any single number.

The code is the manifesto. The methodology is the point. Whether or not "substrate monism" turns out to be the right unification story, treating a theoretical proposal as a computational hypothesis — a commitment that one library reproduces a target table without surgery — turns ontology claims into engineering claims. You either ship the library or you don't.

Point your favorite AI/LLM at our repo and run the code! 

Open source, MIT license:
- Library: https://lnkd.in/gqvUCw2N
- Paper: https://lnkd.in/gbhdgP7K
- Paper DOI: 10.5281/zenodo.20057423

Substrate Monism: One Algebra, Seven Vocabularies, All Forces
Substrate Monism: One Algebra, Seven Vocabularies, All Forces
zenodo.org

Askar Sinchev
4mo  Edited

Our article has been published in Algorithms (MDPI), a Q2 journal in Computer Science:

“Indexed Subset Construction: A Structured Algorithmic Framework”

The work explores structured combinatorial construction, indexed subset generation, and constrained search architectures for exact reasoning tasks under high combinatorial complexity.

Rather than making generalized claims about NP-complete problems, the paper focuses on controlled generation and organization of combinatorial search spaces through index-governed structures and admissibility constraints.

This publication reflects part of our broader ongoing research into architectures for exact and tightly controlled computational reasoning, including their potential relevance for AI systems operating under combinatorial and multi-constraint conditions.

https://lnkd.in/dG8_szBh
